### FND-120

**The AI phone's pairing lapsed after 7 days and the app blamed Wireless debugging** (high defect, open)

On 4 Nov the AI phone connected at 11:32 and refused every connection from 12:02 on: its TLS handshake ends with CERTIFICATE_UNKNOWN, so the phone no longer accepts the stack's key. The pairing dates from 28 Oct 11:54, 7 days before, and 7 days is Android's default for adb_allowed_connection_time. That timing is the evidence; the phone's own list can't be read without the connection, and one write-up says each connection renews the 7 days. The app said 'No wireless debugging port answered', and the owner's Approve all failed all 10 cleanup steps in turn, each with that text.

- Evidence: `adb trace 4 Nov 13:25: tls_connection.cpp 'Post-handshake SSL_peek failed [SSLV3_ALERT_CERTIFICATE_UNKNOWN]' on port 42035`; `/srv/ai/phone/log.jsonl: pair 28 Oct 11:54:50; connect 4 Nov 11:32:57 on port 42035; 10 phone_cleanup results 13:19 to 13:25`
- Affects: [PHN-24](#phn-24), [PHN-25](#phn-25)
- Recommendation: Pair again; switch the limit off as part of Start in AI mode; name a refused connection; let approved steps wait.

### FND-118

**A breaking story reaches the stack about 80 minutes after it is published** (medium risk, open)

The Macusani story was published by three outlets between 12:00 and 12:01 on 2 Nov; the stack raised it at 13:21. The web is looked at every 10 minutes, so the lag is the news search's (Google News) listing. From the raised event the stack took 3 to 4.5 minutes to the answer on the owner's phone, whose message carried only the title.

- Evidence: `missions.sqlite bus 262 and firings 3`; `notifications.jsonl 152 and 153`; `docs/PROBES.md P6`
- Affects: [TSK-12](#tsk-12)
- Recommendation: Add the issuers' own feeds (press-release wires such as GlobeNewswire, the companies' news pages) for the watched subjects, raising a story from one primary source; the answer goes in the alert's message (done).

### FND-18

**Memory is the top risk and nothing budgets it** (medium risk, open)

The review named RAM the biggest operational risk; the host still drops to about 0.45 GB free with Claude sessions and builds running, WSL calls time out, and each new service (and the swarm design's Ollama embeddings) adds memory without a budget across services.

- Evidence: `docs/SWARM-PHASE0.md: 6.1 GiB used, mem_limits add up to 23.8 GiB against the 10 GiB cap`; `docs/REVIEW-2026-10-27.md Verdict`; `docs/DOGFOOD.md item 7`; `this session: repeated WSAETIMEDOUT at 0.47 GB free`
- Affects: [CST-14](#cst-14), [SWM-74](#swm-74)
- Recommendation: A memory budget table (service, limit, measured p95) checked against the 10 GB cap, and the 32 GB RAM upgrade (about 70 EUR) as a shopping proposal.

### FND-01

**The phone check still expects the screen to stay awake** (high contradiction, fixed)

Since DEC-08 the AI phone's screen sleeps after 30 s, but the phone check fails when the screen is not awake ("the wake loop is not working"), so it raises a false alert every week; the guide still tells the owner to switch on Stay awake and says the screen stays on while connected.

- Evidence: `ops/phone_agents.py:266-268 (mWakefulness != Awake is a fault)`; `ops/phone_agents.py:12-14 (docstring)`; `images/mission-control/guide/17-ai-phone.md: 'The screen stays on while the AI phone is connected'`; `images/mission-control/guide/18-crew.md: Phone check 'the screen-awake loop'`; `images/phone/phone.py let_screen_sleep()`
- Affects: [CRW-12](#crw-12), [PHN-13](#phn-13), [APP-14](#app-14)
- Recommendation: Drop the awake check (report the screen state only), and correct the guide's phone and crew chapters.
- Resolved: feat/requirements, 2026-10-29 (docs and the phone check aligned with the requirements)

### FND-02

**Hermes main has a way out through the AI phone** (high incoherence, fixed)

The security model keeps untrusted input, private data and a way out apart, and says Hermes' only way out is e-mail to the owner. Hermes main holds the vault, memory and Gmail read, reads research results, mail bodies and phone screens, and also has phone_url, phone_type and phone_open on an internet-connected phone. Inside a time window the owner opened ("Until I stop it"), those run without asking, so injected text could make Hermes open a URL carrying private data.

- Evidence: `ops/keys.sh: vk-main grants qmd, research, mail, phone`; `docs/SECURITY.md trifecta table and accepted risks (the phone is not mentioned)`; `images/mission-control/guide/14-safety.md: Hermes 'Way out: only to you'`; `images/phone/phone.py guard(): a time window covers phone_url and phone_type`
- Affects: [SEC-04](#sec-04), [PHN-02](#phn-02)
- Recommendation: Decide one of (a) phone_url, phone_type and phone_open always ask like money, (b) a time window does not cover them after the session read mail or research results, (c) accept it as a known risk in SECURITY.md. (a) is the safest and costs a tap per URL or text.
- Resolved: feat/swarm-phase3, 2026-10-29 (DEC-23: typing, URLs and apps always ask; tests/test_phone.py)

### FND-101

**A task's builds were counted twice and nested by guess** (high defect, fixed)

The Mustache trial's second run (task 108) was stopped at 3.4 of its 3 million tokens while it had used about 2.3 million, and its record said 5.2 million. A build's run took its parent by agent name (the live step of the builder), so the tester's build sat under the builder's step and three builds under no step; the budget then counted the nested builds in the trace and again from the builds table, and the trial added up runs whose parents' summaries already held them.

- Evidence: `task 108, 2026-11-02 01:33: its budget of 3,000,000 tokens is spent (3,418,180); recount 2,266,180`; `runtime record: build:14 under task:108:step:3, builds 16, 17, 19 without a parent`
- Affects: [TRL-11](#trl-11), [TRL-09](#trl-09), [TSK-09](#tsk-09)
- Recommendation: A build a step starts names the step's run as its parent; the budget counts a build from the builds table only when its run is outside the trace; a trial counts each model call of the run record once (adding runs' summaries counts some twice, and a summary taken when its run ends misses a build that ends later).
- Resolved: night/w9, 2026-11-02: builds source, staffing.runs_of, conductor.spent, trials.metrics; the recount gives 2,266,180 for task 108

### FND-103

**A wiki compile wrote over the parts of a page it never saw** (high defect, fixed)

The compile showed the model the first 3,000 characters of each page it touches, with no mark, and wrote the model's text over the whole page. The model, told to return each page's full new text, could not keep what it had not seen: from 28 October to 1 November 14 pages of three wikis (the voice speaker, the RAM upgrade, Beta Materials) lost 142 Timeline lines, 264 Open-question lines, 33 Key facts and one contradiction mark, a page's oldest part at each compile. Only the Superseded lines were guarded.

- Evidence: `vault 85d96c0 → f6e1b46 (1 Nov 08:13): projects/ram-upgrade-to-32-gb 6,843 → 4,787 characters; its October Timeline and all four Open questions gone`; `measured 2 Nov over every page's git history: 440 lines lost unseen`
- Affects: [MEM-14](#mem-14)
- Recommendation: Show each page whole up to 12,000 characters with a marked cut past it; a list line past what the model saw comes back under its section; restore the dated Timeline lines from the vault's history (the other sections may have changed since, so they stay in the history and the missions' logs).
- Resolved: night/w14, 2026-11-02: mission_extras PAGE_SEEN and keep_unseen; from the vault's history one dated Timeline line per result a page no longer cited (61 on 9 pages, vault acbd58a; the other lost lines were rewordings of entries still there)

### FND-109

**Model answers cut at their output limit passed as whole, and cuts the check could not see** (high defect, fixed)

No model call read the answer's finish_reason: an answer that stopped at the output limit was parsed (a plan or the desk's JSON cut halfway), stored as a mission result or shown in the chat as finished; a Claude Code run that stopped at its turn limit was read as an empty or partial answer. The truncation check saw only literal slices of 40 or more and only required a reason, so it missed slices by a constant (the Librarian's page and the doctor's evidence in their prompts), slices of 20 to 39 (a mail's date lost its time zone), shell tails (the curator's lint REPORT read through tail -60), SQL lists of the newest rows, and 250 raw slices with a reason someone reads. A reason on the line above also covered the next cap, which hid four.

- Evidence: `ops/truncation.py report and a scan of 2 Nov: 250 raw read caps, 35 constant slices, 39 short slices, 20 shell and 25 SQL caps`; `ops/missions.py ask_text: res['choices'][0]['message']['content'] with no stop reason`
- Affects: [QA-16](#qa-16), [QA-21](#qa-21)
- Recommendation: Read every answer through textcap.answer(); refuse a Claude run stopped at its turn limit; mark every cut someone reads (cut, tail, fit, more, first); remove the caps nothing needs; have the check see every kind of cut, fail on an unmarked one and let one reason cover one cap.
- Resolved: night/w16, 2026-11-02: textcap fit, more, first, answer; builds.claude_text; about 200 cuts marked, 6 removed, 4 refused instead; ops/truncation.py sees slices from 20 and by constant, shell, SQL and model answers

### FND-116

**One unreadable request stopped the AI phone for a day** (high defect, fixed)

At 08:20 on 2 Nov the RAM upgrade's shopping agent asked the AI phone to look the kit up in the Amazon app. It wrote the request with the helper it uses for proposals and purchase approvals, readable by its own user only; the phone's service runs as another user, could not read it, and its control loop read every request inside one error handler, so each pass stopped there: the phone stayed disconnected, did nothing and kept no state for eight and a half hours (Home showed 'AI phone · Not connected'; no alert). A purchase on the phone would have failed the same way. The service could not even remove the file (another user's, in a sticky folder).

- Evidence: `/srv/ai/phone/state.json: connected False, error Permission denied: /phone/control/f31b6883f88646de.json`; `ops/shopping.py save_json: tmp.chmod(0o600) for every file, the phone's control requests included`; `the journey checks' route check: the card AI phone did not open its page on a tap`
- Affects: [SHP-07](#shp-07)
- Recommendation: Write the phone's requests readable by the phone; read each request on its own, report one it cannot read once and go on; alert when the AI phone stays disconnected.
- Resolved: fix/phone-control, 2026-11-02: shopping.to_phone (0644) for shop_look and shop_open; phone.take skips and reports what it cannot read or delete; the stuck request moved aside and the phone reconnected at 16:55. The alert for a long disconnection: crew.phone_down (fix/p6), an alert after 30 min disconnected or 10 min silent unless stopped

### FND-119

**A cold start of the PC left the stack down for three hours and every job failing** (high defect, fixed)

On 4 Nov the PC started cold for the first time since the stack's scheduled jobs were built (every earlier boot was a restart of WSL with Windows already up, containerd ready in under 4 s). containerd took over 15 s to open its database; Docker allows it 15 s and that cannot be configured. Three such starts hit systemd's limit of 3 a minute, which is final: Docker stayed down from 08:40 until it was started by hand at 11:32. The runner did not ask: it started the morning's four missions at once and marked them failed. Every scheduled job did the same (skills-sync 30 times, the backup, the Checker, the doctor), and the jobs the PC slept through all fired in its first minutes. The health board also called jobs overdue for the hours the PC was off. The owner got an alert per symptom and none that named Docker; a Hermes-routed notice could not reach anyone because Hermes runs in Docker too.

- Evidence: `journalctl --user -u docker -b: 'waiting for response from boltdb open', 'timeout waiting for containerd to start' at 08:40:03, 08:40:22, 08:40:39, 'Start request repeated too quickly' at 08:40:42`; `missions.sqlite runs of 4 Nov 08:40-08:41: 4 failed, 'Cannot connect to the Docker daemon' or 'no answer'`; `public/events.jsonl 08:40:40: 'Memory gatekeeper: did not run when scheduled' 14 hours after the PC was switched off`
- Affects: [PLT-28](#plt-28), [PLT-29](#plt-29), [PLT-30](#plt-30)
- Recommendation: Docker retries for ever; one gate for the runner and every job; catch-ups one at a time; no alerts during a start; one message naming the cause when the stack stays down.
- Resolved: fix/boot, 2026-11-04: ops/systemd/docker-boot.conf (PLT-29), ops/stack_gate.py on every timer's unit and in the runner (PLT-28), ops/stack_ready.py opens the gate and reports each start (PLT-30); the failing boot rehearsed with containerd's database held locked (docs/PROBES.md P8). A real cold start of the PC is the remaining proof, and its result is on the Stack page.

### FND-122

**Onboarding refused any repository over 150 source files, and told nobody** (high defect, fixed)

ops/understand.sh refused a repository with more than 150 source files. The limit came in with commit ba35be9 on 25 Oct 2026, from a coding session, to protect the shared ChatGPT limit; the owner never asked for it and no requirement or test named it, while SN-14 says the stack can study any repository. The runner started the job and logged 'Analysing' without looking at the result, the app promised 'you get a message', which no code sent, even on success, and the repo page had no running or failed state. So on 6 Nov the button did nothing the owner could see, and Hermes could only say 'not yet confirmed'.

- Evidence: `journalctl: understand-1793950559 and understand-1793951026, 177 source files, refused within 2 s`; `ops/understand.sh, ops/ua_enrich.py, ops/ua_digest.py: no call that sends a message`; `/srv/ai/missions/public/events.jsonl: only 'Analysing' events, no end`; `ops/understand.sh, in git since ba35be9: nothing in requirements/ or tests/ named MAX_FILES`
- Affects: [BLD-26](#bld-26)
- Recommendation: One restartable job with no size limit, its steps on the repo page and in repos_overview, a real message when it ends.
- Resolved: fix/boot, 2026-11-06: ops/onboard.py replaces ops/understand.sh (tests/test_onboard.py)

### FND-124

**The NAS's real address reached GitHub, and the public copy told strangers more than it had to** (high defect, fixed)

eval/bench-v1.yaml held the NAS's real LAN address as a regular expression, with a backslash before each dot, in all 8 public commits. The map replaces a plain address and the scan reads plain text, so neither saw it, and a first audit on 6 Nov missed it for the same reason. It is reachable only from the owner's own network. The name, e-mail, tailnet and PC name are in no commit, also with backslashes taken out. Beyond that, the map held identifiers only: the placeholder kept real last numbers, the README named the NAS model, and the public copy showed the topics and companies the owner follows, router and phone models, and 16 records of real runs. The owner noticed on 6 Nov 2026.

- Evidence: `every public commit's files, backslashes removed, against the map on 6 Nov 2026: only eval/bench-v1.yaml, line 116`; `README.md and docs/HOME-VOICE.md at 8328817`; `eval/results/ at 8328817: 19 files, 3 cited by requirements`
- Affects: [SEC-19](#sec-19)
- Recommendation: The scan reads text without backslashes; the map holds what a stranger should not learn; run records stay out; one fresh public history, on the owner's word.
- Resolved: fix/boot, 2026-11-06: ops/publish.py scan and LEFT_OUT and push --fresh, the private map, and every landing runs the scan (tests/test_publish.py); the owner chose on 6 Nov 2026 to keep the old public commits

### FND-22

**Agents talking to each other would cross the trust boundaries** (high incoherence, fixed)

The security model keeps private data, untrusted content and ways out apart per agent (the scout reads the web and holds nothing private; Hermes main holds private data and has no way out). The swarm design lets every agent read every artifact and message every other agent. Private data could then travel from Hermes main to an agent with a way out (the scout's fetch and browser, the AI phone), and web content could reach Hermes main inside a message whose origin is an agent, not the web. The design's provenance tags say where content came from, but nothing stops private content from leaving.

- Evidence: `design doc, section 'Safety, security and cost guardrails' (provenance, no flow rule)`; `requirements SEC-03, SEC-04, SEC-05`; `ops/keys.sh: vk-scout grants fetch and browser, vk-main grants mail and phone`
- Affects: [SWM-09](#swm-09), [SWM-10](#swm-10), [SWM-15](#swm-15), [SWM-67](#swm-67), [SEC-04](#sec-04)
- Recommendation: Information-flow labels on every message, evidence entry and artifact, carried into whatever is derived from them and enforced by the Swarm service when it delivers (SWM-67); room:all and artifact search respect the labels.
- Resolved: swarm phase 4 (2026-10-30): images/swarm sensitivity labels and the way-out rule (SWM-67); confirmed 2026-11-01 by tests/test_swarm_board.py::test_contact_policy_and_private_content and tests/test_swarm_ledger.py::test_private_artifacts_never_reach_an_agent_with_a_way_out

### FND-37

**A deployed agent change did not reach its agent** (high defect, fixed)

Build #10 (agent change #380) deployed the maintainability review's new standing instructions, but the swarm service still served the old card: it reads config/agents.yaml only when it starts, through a single-file bind mount that keeps the file git replaced. The context pack, and so the agent's prompt, had no instructions until a restart.

- Evidence: `2026-10-30 ~07:30 after build #10 deployed 649338a: the file in aistack-swarm-1 had 0 instructions, the repo's had 3; after docker restart aistack-swarm-1: 3, and lessons_text('maintainability') starts with them`
- Affects: [SWM-18](#swm-18), [SWM-102](#swm-102), [PLT-08](#plt-08)
- Recommendation: A deploy that changes config/agents.yaml restarts the swarm service.
- Resolved: swarm/phase5-cards, 2026-10-30 (tests/test_deploy.py::test_a_card_change_restarts_the_swarm_service_so_the_agents_get_it); the live service was restarted by hand at ~07:31

### FND-38

**Text the owner reads or types was cut silently all over the stack** (high defect, fixed)

The truncation audit of 2026-10-30 found 556 text caps of 40 or more characters and 16 CSS ellipsis or line clamps. About 230 cut what the owner reads or types, with no mark: log lines on the agent pages (300 characters, then one line), errors in events and Telegram (120 to 300), review findings (600), the build diff (1,500 lines), tool results in run logs (3,000), the phone's approval cards (what will be typed or opened: 200), mail Trash samples (sender 40, subject 60), and the owner's own input (build descriptions 8,000, Chatroom posts 4,000, reasons 300, mission goals 4,000, titles 100).

- Evidence: `owner's screenshot 2026-10-30 07:21 (Swarm sync, Latest run: every line ends in '…')`; `python3 ops/truncation.py report`
- Affects: [APP-19](#app-19), [APP-20](#app-20), [QA-16](#qa-16)
- Recommendation: Show text whole, refuse over-long input, and require a named reason for every remaining cap.
- Resolved: fix/no-truncation, 2026-10-30 (tests/test_truncation.py)

**The swarm's views could not be reached on a phone** (high defect, fixed)

navHtml(bottom) left Swarm out of the phone's tab bar, and the only way in was a banner at the end of Code's collapsed 'The stack suggests'. The owner, who uses the app on a phone, looked for the Timeline, Map, Chatroom and Agents views built on 2026-10-30 and found the app 'looks the same'.

**Start mission threw before sending anything, since 28 October** (high defect, fixed)

The owner's second try at 17:17 spun as well. The app reported "watch is not defined at f.onsubmit (app.js:1499)": commit 4063a90 (shopping missions, 2026-10-28) put the price-watch lines into the mission settings form's handler instead of the New mission form's, so Start mission threw right after showing its spinner, for every type, and the settings form read a type field it does not have. Missions created since came through chat.

### FND-53

**The app's errors lived only in the container's log** (high defect, fixed)

The app reported each error to /api/clienterror, which only printed it; a container recreate (every deploy of mission-control) wiped the history, nothing raised it, and the Start mission error of FND-52 went unseen for two days until the owner hit it. At the review there was no record left of how often it had happened.

- Evidence: `images/mission-control/server.py api_clienterror (log line only)`; `docker logs aistack-mission-control-1 (history begins at the last recreate)`
- Affects: [APP-24](#app-24)

### FND-54

**No check catches a name the app's scripts never define** (high defect, fixed)

The app's four scripts share one global scope and have no build step, so an undefined name (the watch of FND-52) passes every test that only reads the code and fails only when the owner taps. A lint of the four scripts joined in load order reports it at app.js:1499 on the code before the fix and nothing on the code after it.

- Evidence: `python3 ops/app_lint.py on 4063a90 reports app.js:1499 no-undef 'watch'`; `ops/check.sh had no step for the app's scripts`
- Affects: [APP-24](#app-24)
- Recommendation: Lint the scripts for undefined names in every landing and image build, and keep a click-through harness.
- Resolved: fix/ux-review, 2026-10-30: ops/app_lint.py in ops/check.sh, ESLint in the builder and CI images, ops/ui_crawl.js

### FND-76

**The nightly backup failed on one unreadable file again** (high defect, fixed)

On 2026-10-31 at 03:32 the backup failed: rsync could not read a file a container had written under its own user in /srv/ai/workbench (scratch copies of repositories), exited 23, and the whole backup failed. PLT-13 was marked implemented, but it had been built as one exclusion for the 29 Oct case (the AI phone's key), verified by inspection only. The health board raised it (crew: Backup failed); the newest backup was a day old.

- Evidence: `journalctl --user -u backup (2026-10-31 03:32): Permission denied (13) on workbench/ai-stack.1/.serena/project.yml, code 23`; `/srv/ai/state/backups.jsonl: last backup 2026-10-30 03:32`
- Affects: [PLT-13](#plt-13)

### FND-77

**A weekly briefing task wrote "blocked" on its first run and looked like it waited for Monday** (high defect, fixed)

Task #79 (a weekly e-mail of Chinese automotive news) ran at once (mission run 52), but the desk kept only 36 hours of the week its searches asked for (231 items, 3 recent, 0 chosen), sent its Chinese queries to US-English Google News with when:1d added to their own when:7d, and the brief's notes were the intake's plan for agents (Main, Scout, Desk), so the writer reported its research and e-mail blocked. The task never asked the desk for e-mail, the price table carried the copper brief's Sprott note, and the board showed the task only as scheduled.

- Evidence: `/srv/ai/desk/weekly-chinese-automotive-news-email/last.log (2026-10-31 16:54): 231 items collected, 3 new in 36 h; triage kept 0`; `topics/weekly-chinese-automotive-news-email/TOPIC.md notes (Main must enable Task #79 ...)`; `missions.sqlite runs id 52: Briefing blocked: no source material supplied`

### FND-80

**A job search found no openings because the stack had no job board** (high defect, fixed)

On 30 Oct the owner's job hunt (a senior engineering role near home) ran first as a news briefing: Google News over 36 hours holds no job ads, so it collected 0 items and mailed an empty report with a Mercedes share price. Run again as deep research, the scouts made 97 web searches and read 47 pages in 16 minutes and confirmed two openings, in Ergersheim and Kassel. General web search finds job ads badly and the big portals refuse automated readers; the federal job board's open search returns current openings within 50 km of home.

- Evidence: `/srv/ai/desk/job-report/last.log: 0 items collected`; `mission run 47: 97 web_search, 47 fetch calls, 489,941 tokens`; `rest.arbeitsagentur.de .../pc/v6/jobs?was=<role>&wo=<town>&umkreis=50: 5 openings (2026-10-31)`
- Affects: [MIS-11](#mis-11), [MIS-21](#mis-21)

The re-run (mission run 55) still made 99 web searches and no job-board call, because the scout's brief did not name the board; fix/now-card added it to prompts/scout.md BRIEF (replay 4/4 valid, judge 3-1) and replays of a standing brief now bring their requests (ops/prompts.py task_of)

### FND-84

**The first run started by an event deadlocked the Conductor** (high defect, fixed)

The Conductor's worker ticks while holding its lock, and start() takes the same lock to open a run. The lock was a plain threading.Lock, so the first occurrence started from the worker (an event's, or a schedule's) blocked the worker for good: the trigger proof's task #82 stayed running with no run, and no plan task could move until a restart. The tests called tick() without the lock, so they never saw it. The task was also marked running before its run existed, so a stop in between left it running with nothing to run.

- Evidence: `2026-11-01 01:55: trigger proof, task #82 status running, no task_occ row, no Conductor log line after the event`; `ops/conductor.py: lock = threading.Lock(); worker(): with lock: tick(c); start(): with lock:`
- Affects: [TSK-21](#tsk-21), [TSK-22](#tsk-22)
- Recommendation: A re-entrant lock; the run first, then the claim; recover() puts a claimed but never started task back to waiting; a test ticks under the lock.
- Resolved: fix/conductor-lock, 2026-11-01: ops/conductor.py lock (RLock), tick(), recover(); tests/test_conductor.py

### FND-88

**Requests that came together mixed up their reads of the run record** (high defect, fixed)

Mission Control's run record (runrecord.py) kept one SQLite connection for all of its request threads. Two requests at once, such as an agent's page asking for its runs and its room, could interleave on it: sqlite's 'bad parameter or other API misuse' or a run read back as None, an HTTP 500 the page showed as an error. The journey check found it on every new agent page; a test with eight threads reading while one writes failed on it 3 times in 3.

- Evidence: `ops/journeys.py routes, 1 Nov 2026: HTTP 500 /api/swarm/timeline and /api/swarm/room on #/swarm/agent/*`; `mission-control log: sqlite3.InterfaceError at runrecord.name_of; AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'startswith'`
- Affects: [OBS-01](#obs-01)
- Recommendation: One connection per thread; writes stay behind the one lock.
- Resolved: fix/agents-one-page, 2026-11-01: images/mission-control/runrecord.py db(); tests/test_runtime.py

### FND-90

**The first trial failed in 3 minutes on a builder step that only planned** (high defect, fixed)

Trial semver (1 Nov 2026, task #88): the intake split the coding work into three builder steps (read the spec and plan; implement with tests; run the tests and fix). Each builder step is a build, a build that commits nothing fails, so the first step failed although the builder mapped every rule of the specification to tests and named its traps; its retry (build #12) did the same and the task failed. The third step would have failed the same way whenever its tests already passed. The harness also lost the builds' numbers (a failed step's retry keeps only the newest) and reported none.

- Evidence: `/srv/vault/trials/semver/semver-20261101-1128.md`; `builds #11 and #12: 'the builder made no commit'`; `task_steps of task 88`
- Affects: [TRL-10](#trl-10), [TRL-06](#trl-06)
- Recommendation: Consecutive builder steps become one build with stages; the harness finds a trial's builds by its repository.
- Resolved: feat/trials, 2026-11-01: ops/conductor.py fold_builders(); ops/trials.py build_ids(repo); tests/test_conductor.py

### FND-94

**A repository with only a tests folder ran no checks and passed** (high defect, fixed)

The builder's default check ran `ls tests test_*.py */test_*.py`, which fails when any one of them is missing, so a repository with a tests folder but no test_*.py at its top said "no automatic checks in this repo" and passed: trial semver's 114 tests never ran in the stack, and the Conductor's fix loop (TRL-08) could never fire on a trial's code.

- Evidence: `builds.checks of build #13 (task #91), 1 Nov 2026: "no automatic checks in this repo / ALL CHECKS PASSED"`
- Affects: [BLD-24](#bld-24), [TRL-08](#trl-08), [TRL-03](#trl-03)
- Recommendation: Find tests as pytest does; run pytest from the sandbox's tools.
- Resolved: night/w1, 2026-11-01: ops/builds.py DEFAULT_CHECK; tests/test_builds.py

### FND-97

**A task its agent could not do was reported done** (high defect, fixed)

Task #104 (install Reddit on the AI phone and find a post) went to the mission agent, which has no phone tools: the intake saw each agent's purpose but not what it can reach. Both steps answered that they were blocked; the Conductor counted them done, the judge rated the result met (its definition of done allowed naming a blocker), and the owner was told the task was done. Its notification linked to the task board, where a finished task sat in a closed section, so the link opened another task.

- Evidence: `task_steps of task 104 (outputs: Step 1 blocked, Step 2 blocked)`; `owner's screenshot, 1 Nov 2026 20:59`
- Affects: [TSK-35](#tsk-35), [APP-35](#app-35), [TSK-17](#tsk-17)
- Recommendation: The intake sees each agent's tools; a step that says it is blocked fails its task with the reason; every task link opens the task's own page.
- Resolved: night/w1, 2026-11-01: ops/tasks.py cards(), ops/conductor.py BLOCKED, prompts/shared.md TASK_STEP, task.js; tests/test_conductor.py, tests/test_tasks.py, tests/test_task_page.py

### FND-99

**A long requirements list stopped the trial's builders before they started** (high defect, fixed)

The Mustache trial of 2 November (task 107) failed four minutes in. The analyst's numbered requirements (about 26,000 characters) went into the tester's and the builder's brief, and the builder refuses a description over 20,000 characters (the owner's limit, APP-20), so both build steps failed twice with that error and the task failed with 0 of 0 points. The Orchestrator's checkpoint between the waves had run and kept the plan.

- Evidence: `task 107 steps 2 and 3, 2026-11-02 01:03: the description is 28,389 characters; the limit is 20,000`
- Affects: [TSK-07](#tsk-07), [TRL-05](#trl-05)
- Recommendation: A step's brief and the earlier steps' results travel apart, as they already did for a Claude task; the builder takes the results as the build's context (80,000 characters, under the one-argument limit of the engine's command line) and the owner's description keeps its limit.
- Resolved: night/w7, 2026-11-02: conductor.split_brief, builds context and brief(); the Mustache trial ran again

### FND-03

**One key, many agents** (medium incoherence, fixed)

"One key per agent" is the least-privilege rule and the basis of cost per run, but vk-mission is used by mission runs and by six background jobs. The inbox digest therefore holds the mission grants (qmd, research, claude, mail), and a task run's cost window includes journal entries, wiki summaries or the self-review that ran at the same time.

- Evidence: `ops/inbox.py:44,108`; `ops/improve.py:213`; `ops/crew.py:799`; `ops/journal.py:336,444`; `ops/wikis.py:83`; `ops/shopping.py:6`; `docs/MISSION-CONTROL.md: 'a run's calls are exactly its key's calls in that window'`
- Affects: [SEC-07](#sec-07), [MIS-16](#mis-16), [SWM-23](#swm-23)
- Recommendation: Give each background job its own key with only the grants it needs (vk-crew for model-only jobs, vk-inbox with mail only), generated from config/agents.yaml once the swarm registry exists (SWM-01).
- Resolved: feat/swarm-phase0, 2026-10-29 (vk-review, vk-inbox, vk-memory, vk-journal, vk-wikis; a test keeps vk-mission to mission runs)

### FND-04

**A second, undocumented delivery path** (medium contradiction, fixed)

The invariant says only the host runner merges and deploys, after the owner's tap, with review and green CI. Most changes so far were landed by interactive Claude Code sessions instead, under a chat approval. They fast-forward main from a worktree, push, and run ops/deploy.py directly, serialised only by a flock convention. That path has no AI review, CI runs only after the deploy, and nothing records the deploy in Mission Control. (ops/deploy.py holds its own lock since 2026-10-29, PLT-10.)

- Evidence: `AGENTS.md: Architecture invariants`; `docs/DOGFOOD.md: 'parallel sessions used ~/.aistack-land.lock by convention'`; `ops/deploy.py: no flock until 2026-10-29`; `git log: 9b1ef1d, 96b0912, a87f69e landed this way on 2026-10-29`; `2026-10-29: 10607d9 landed by a session with a failing test (its script did not stop on the checks; 679a0ce fixed it)`; `2026-10-29: a session deploy printed RESTART_RUNNER, nothing restarted the runner, and the owner's Pause ran on old code until a manual restart`
- Affects: [CST-07](#cst-07), [PLT-10](#plt-10), [BLD-20](#bld-20)
- Recommendation: Make the path explicit (BLD-20), record every deploy as an activity event with its source, and run ops/check.sh before landing (sessions already do, by habit only).
- Resolved: feat/swarm-switch, 2026-10-29 (ops/land.sh: checks on the landing commit, both locks, runner restart, deploys recorded with their source, an activity event with the approval)

### FND-05

**"Everything goes to the ChatGPT subscription" is no longer true** (medium contradiction, fixed)

README, the welcome and safety chapters say every model call goes through the ChatGPT subscription and only that call leaves the house. Claude Code on the Claude subscription now builds by default, reviews ChatGPT builds, runs the health doctor, the maintainability review and Claude tasks, and receives repository code and redacted logs.

- Evidence: `README.md line 4`; `images/mission-control/guide/01-welcome.md 'It costs $0 to run'`; `images/mission-control/guide/14-safety.md 'Your data stays home'`; `ops/builds.py:82 (Claude by default)`
- Affects: [SEC-18](#sec-18), [CST-02](#cst-02)
- Recommendation: Say "your ChatGPT and Claude subscriptions" and list what Anthropic sees (repository code, redacted evidence), in README.md, the guide's welcome, safety and technology chapters, and docs/SECURITY.md.
- Resolved: feat/requirements, 2026-10-29 (docs and the phone check aligned with the requirements)

### FND-10

**The scout's web search package is not where the scout looks** (medium defect, fixed)

ddgs is installed into /opt/hermes/.venv, but the scout profile runs another interpreter and logs "ddgs package is not installed" (540 times in 2 days), so web search takes a slower fallback. Found by the health doctor (proposal 27).

- Evidence: `images/hermes/Dockerfile:7`; `config/hermes/configure-scout.sh:28`; `docker logs aistack-hermes-1 --since 48h: 540 lines`
- Affects: [RES-02](#res-02)
- Recommendation: Build proposal 27 (install into the interpreter Hermes uses, or point the search backend at it).
- Resolved: fix/scout-search, 2026-10-30. Cause: on 2026-10-28 Hermes made its own dependency environment (/opt/data/installs/..., for the anthropic extra); a launch then uses only that environment and drops the image's .venv, where the Dockerfile installs ddgs (searches had worked until then, the last ddgs result on 2026-10-27). configure-scout.sh now installs Hermes' own ddgs extra into its environment (tests/test_scout_search.py)

### FND-100

**The checkpoint took a cut result for a broken one** (medium defect, fixed)

In the Mustache trial's second run (task 108) the Orchestrator's checkpoint read only the first 4,000 characters of the analyst's requirements, with no sign that the rest was cut, judged the list broken off and added a step to finish it. It also rewrote the tester's and the builder's briefs to wait for that step, but an added step could only follow other steps, so they ran beside it instead. Its third look dropped the builder's step while a check had sent it back for a fix round (a pending step that had run), and the run ended over its token budget (3.4 of 3 million) with the code at 136 of 136 on the key.

- Evidence: `task 108 checkpoint 1, 2026-11-02 01:22: the visible analyst result ends mid-requirement; step 9 added after step 1`
- Affects: [TRL-08](#trl-08)
- Recommendation: The checkpoint reads each result's start with the cut marker (how much is missing, and that the next steps get it whole); an added step can name the steps not yet started that wait for it (before); a step that ran before (a fix round, a next attempt) is shown as running again and is never dropped or rewritten. The prompt's replay passed (new 2, old 0, equal 2).
- Resolved: night/w8, 2026-11-02: conductor.checkpoint (SEEN, before), TASK_CHECKPOINT

### FND-102

**The judge could not see what the agents read** (medium defect, fixed)

The Mustache trial's third run (task 109) scored 136 of 136 with a clean audit, and the judge rated its clean-room point not met: it rested on the builder's word, as the judge saw no record of what was read. The run record had every command and file read of the task's agents.

- Evidence: `task 109 judgement, 2026-11-02 02:04: without a verifiable file-access record`
- Affects: [TSK-17](#tsk-17)
- Recommendation: The judge's measured section lists the run record's tool calls of the task's runs (agent, tool, the start of its argument; 80 named, the task page has all); re-judged with it, the clean-room point was met. TASK_JUDGE says so; replay passed (equal 3).
- Resolved: night/w10, 2026-11-02: staffing.calls_of and evidence_of, TASK_JUDGE

### FND-104

**Every step still gets earlier results copied whole** (medium risk, fixed)

After FND-99 the earlier results travel apart from a step's brief, but still whole: 80,000 characters for a build, a Claude task's context, and for a Hermes step the whole prompt as one command-line argument, which Linux refuses past 128 KiB (MAX_ARG_STRLEN). A Claude step's brief is sliced at 19,000 characters without a mark.

- Evidence: `ops/conductor.py run_hermes: hermes -z <prompt>`; `ops/conductor.py run_claude: head[:19000]`
- Affects: [MEM-15](#mem-15)
- Recommendation: Pass by reference past a budget (MEM-15), Hermes' prompt through a file, and a size contract test through every adapter.
- Resolved: night/w17, 2026-11-02: conductor handoff and result_file (by reference past HANDOFF), the OpenCode step's results folder, run_hermes refuses a prompt over ARG_BYTES

### FND-105

**Most cuts a model reads do not show themselves** (medium risk, fixed)

Of 96 places that cut text going to a model (cap: prompt), 92 slice it without a mark, so the model takes the start for the whole: the cause of FND-100 and FND-103. Among them the wiki context of a refinement (8,000), the mission plan's notes (3,000), a journal's result (3,000) and the inbox's mail body (20,000).

- Evidence: `ops/truncation.py report, 2 Nov: 96 prompt caps, 4 through textcap`
- Affects: [MEM-16](#mem-16), [QA-16](#qa-16)
- Recommendation: A prompt cap goes through textcap.cut or tail, enforced by ops/truncation.py; the long ones first.
- Resolved: night/w16, 2026-11-02: the second truncation audit; every prompt cap marked, the check refuses an unmarked one

### FND-107

**Long-term memory repeats itself and keeps what belongs elsewhere** (medium incoherence, fixed)

Hindsight held 222 facts on 2 November, up from 120 on 30 October: the voice-reply preference up to five times, facts in Spanish and German, about 35 facts on agent runs (journals hold those, MEM-06), and passing states (a setup 'incomplete') with no end. The weekly curator's 55 forget suggestions of 30 October wait for the owner's tap; none was taken.

- Evidence: `hindsight memory_units 2 Nov: 65 world, 74 experience, 83 observation`; `crew/memory.json: 55 suggestions, 30 Oct`
- Affects: [MEM-18](#mem-18)
- Recommendation: Reconcile at write (a repeat confirms, not adds), English facts, agent-run facts kept out; the owner decides whether exact duplicates and test noise may be retired softly without a tap (docs/MEMORY.md D2).
- Resolved: night/w17, 2026-11-02: the Memory Keeper (ops/keeper.py) behind its trial; Hindsight's retain mission asks for English and no agents' runs; DEC-40

### FND-110

**A compiled wiki page lost the day it was made** (medium defect, fixed)

The second truncation audit (827455e) put a cap's reason comment in the middle of the wiki compile's page metadata, so the key after it, created, became part of the comment: every page a mission, a briefing or a refinement compiled from then on was written without its created date (24 pages on 2 Nov, three wikis). No test read created back; found while building the task page.

- Evidence: `ops/mission_extras.py compile_into: '"sources": sorted(...), # every source stays (it was the newest 30) "created": old.get("created", today),'`; `vault: 24 pages of the RAM, voice speaker and Battery recycling wikis without created after 06:00 on 2 Nov`
- Affects: [MEM-14](#mem-14)
- Recommendation: Put the key back before the comment, test that a compiled page keeps its created date, and restore the 24 pages' dates from the vault's history.
- Resolved: task/pages, 2026-11-02: created back in compile_into; tests/test_librarian.py checks it; the 24 pages' dates restored from their earlier versions (or the day the page was first committed)

### FND-111

**A mission's research was filed under another task's scout step** (medium defect, fixed)

Hermes runs its agents in one resident process, so a scout run cannot be told who started it and the run record guessed: a live Conductor step of the same agent first. On 2 Nov the RAM mission asked the scout three questions while the Battery recycling briefing's scout steps 6 and 7 ran: the answers were filed under those steps, so the briefing's page and swarm map listed geizhals.de RAM searches as its top source and counted their tokens. Of 35 scout runs on record that answered a research call, 3 sat under the wrong task, 7 under no run and 24 under the other of two parallel sessions of the same mission run.

- Evidence: `runtime.db: hermes:scout:8033d389..., f1f1afa9..., aad86ca4... ref task:79:step:6/7, titled with questions the RAM mission's hermes:mission:077ce7f5... asked through research_research seconds before`; `images/mission-control/runrecord.py _infer_ref: the live task step of the same agent first`
- Affects: [OBS-18](#obs-18), [APP-36](#app-36)
- Recommendation: Match a scout run to the research call that asked its question; re-file the runs on record; redraw the briefing's map and recount its tokens.
- Resolved: task/scout-ref, 2026-11-02: runrecord._asked_by and reparent_research; the 35 runs re-filed, task 79's map and tokens recounted

### FND-115

**A task's own words could widen its page on a phone** (medium defect, fixed)

The task page and the task card quote the owner's words ("You wrote: ...") on a line that could not break: task 113's words held a 350-character link, the phone widened the page to fit and the agents' map drew to that width, so the page scrolled sideways by 2,417 px at 375 px. Found by the journey checks' route check.

- Evidence: `python3 ops/journeys.py: #/task/113 scrolls sideways at 375 px by 2417 px`; `docs/PROBES.md, the sweep`
- Affects: [APP-36](#app-36)
- Recommendation: Let the line wrap anywhere, as every line of the owner's or an agent's text does.
- Resolved: ops/battle2, 2026-11-02: the line wraps; the route check passes all 115 routes

### FND-117

**A task whose planning a restart cut off waited forever** (medium defect, fixed)

Task 93 (the Acme Mining rule's alert of 1 Nov) sat in 'planning' for 27 hours: the planner marks a task planning before its model call, a runner restart (a deploy) cut the call off, and the worker only looks for new tasks.

- Evidence: `missions.sqlite tasks 93: status planning since 2026-11-01 13:32`; `docs/PROBES.md P6`
- Affects: [TSK-03](#tsk-03)
- Recommendation: At the worker's start plan such a task again, or fail it with the reason when it is too old to matter.
- Resolved: fix/p6, 2026-11-02: tasks.recover at the worker's start: within 6 h planned again, older failed with Plan it again

### FND-12

**Caps the owner rejected are still in the code** (medium contradiction, fixed)

Per-agent caps on open proposals were added on 2026-10-29 and rejected by the owner the same day (DEC-10); they stay in improve.py until the swarm lifecycle replaces them.

- Evidence: `ops/improve.py CAPS`; `DEC-10`
- Affects: [CRW-06](#crw-06), [SWM-08](#swm-08), [SWM-35](#swm-35)
- Recommendation: Remove the caps when SWM-04/SWM-08 land (phase 1 of the swarm design), or earlier on the owner's word.
- Resolved: feat/swarm-switch, 2026-10-29 (caps, title matching and stale closures removed; the Ledger ranks instead)

### FND-121

**A newsletter on a watched topic never reached that topic, and the digest judged mail by its subject alone** (medium defect, fixed)

The owner asked on 4 Nov whether everything in his copper newsletter reaches the wiki. Of the 33 mails the digest saw that day, 1 named a watched topic in its text: a ratings site, one line saying Acme Mining has a new analyst rating, no fact. 3 more matched only because 'mg' was read as the car maker MG. Over the five recorded days (148 mails) it was 2: that one and A markets site's weekly watchlist, the only mail with numbers on copper companies. In 60 days Gmail held no newsletter about copper as such: the 9 other hits are the stack's own briefing mailed to him. MAX_FILE (5) was never reached (0, 1, 3, 0, 1 picks). The filter was the weak part: the model sees sender and subject only, so it picked a ratings site' 'A stock on your watchlist' three times on a guess, and whatever it picked went to the general vault, while a topic's wiki is compiled only from that topic's reports. The listing was the silent risk: it took the newest 40 of a two-day window that held 61, with 35 new on 2 Nov. Gmail's own search is no substitute: an OR search for Acme Mining missed the a ratings site mail that a narrower search found.

- Evidence: `/srv/ai/state/prompt-cases/inbox.PICK.jsonl: 148 rows over 5 runs`; `mail_read of all 148 against each active TOPIC.md's names, 4 Nov`; `/srv/ai/missions/crew/inbox.json: filed 1 of 33`; `mail_search newer_than:60d copper terms: 8 a markets site, 9 AI Stack`
- Affects: [CRW-13](#crw-13), [MIS-28](#mis-28), [MEM-04](#mem-04)
- Recommendation: Search each newsletter's own text for the topic's names, check matches against its key questions, hand them to the topic. MEM-04's rule against private wikis meant personal mail: a topic takes only mail with a List-Unsubscribe header, never protected mail, and its text reaches the wiki only through the report, so its wiki stays shared.
- Resolved: feat/topic-mail, 2026-11-04: ops/inbox.py hand_over and settle, images/desk/desk.py from_mail, the topic page's From your e-mail, the listing raised to the tool's 100 and reaching back to the last finished run

### FND-123

**Limits nobody chose dropped work without a word** (medium defect, fixed)

An audit after FND-122 found more of the kind. A drafted briefing checked only its first 8 feeds, 15 symbols and 10 filings and removed the rest as if they had failed. A mission kept 10 triggers and 10 tickers, the watchlist 15 entries, and a fan-out the first 8 items, all without a word. Past 10 Board messages a day the owner's phone stayed silent, and an event dropped from a task's queue reached only the log. The truncation check could not see them: it ignored slices under 20. And an attachment's sheets past 10 and rows past 2,000 were left out unmarked. Nothing listed the stack's limits.

- Evidence: `images/desk/desk.py, ops/missions.py, ops/mission_extras.py, ops/conductor.py, ops/swarm_client.py at 3a94403`; `ops/truncation.py at 3a94403: MIN = 20`
- Affects: [QA-22](#qa-22)
- Recommendation: Refuse with the limit named, say what was left out, or drop the limit; tag every limit and generate the list.
- Resolved: fix/boot, 2026-11-06: the limits refuse or say so, ops/limits.py and docs/LIMITS.md (tests/test_limits.py)

### FND-14

**The mail connector can strand an approval like the phone did** (medium defect, fixed)

The first-Trash approval uses the same pending/decisions loop the AI phone used before its fix on 2026-10-29; a restart of the mail container (every deploy that touches it) leaves the card unanswerable.

- Evidence: `images/mail/mail.py:27-40,308 (pending/ and decisions/, no sweep)`; `images/phone/phone.py sweep_orphans() (the phone's fix)`
- Affects: [CNX-06](#cnx-06), [SWM-19](#swm-19)
- Recommendation: Port sweep_orphans() to the mail connector, or move both to the swarm's persistent waits (SWM-19).
- Resolved: feat/reliability, 2026-11-01: images/mail/mail.py WAITING and sweep_orphans() (at the start and every status pass); tests/test_mail.py

### FND-17

**Seven ways to send a Telegram message** (medium incoherence, fixed)

Only the health board applies once-per-issue; other jobs send Telegram messages through their own code, so there is no single place for a notification policy (quiet hours, preferences, dedupe) that SN-18 needs. (The maintainability proposal to route them through notify.sh was dismissed on 2026-10-29 as low value; seen from the notification policy it is not.)

- Evidence: `ops/memory_review.py:41`; `ops/skills_sync.py:45`; `ops/missions.py:107`; `ops/mission_extras.py`; `ops/notify.sh`; `ops/vault-ingest.sh`; `ops/vault-lint.sh`
- Affects: [OBS-16](#obs-16), [SWM-59](#swm-59)
- Recommendation: One notify function with the policy, used by every sender; reopen proposal 10 in that light.
- Resolved: feat/phase0, 2026-10-31: ops/notify.py is the one path (preferences, quiet hours, once per key, log); every sender goes through it, and tests/test_notify.py stops a new one that bypasses it

### FND-23

**The swarm would start jobs, which only the runner may do** (medium contradiction, fixed)

The design wakes an agent by starting its unit (systemctl --user start crew-<id>), a Kanban task or a Claude task from the Swarm service. The invariant says only the runner (ops/missions.py on the host) runs jobs and validates every request, and a container cannot start the host's systemd units anyway.

- Evidence: `design doc, 'Delivery and waking' and the harness adapter table`; `AGENTS.md: Architecture invariants`; `requirements SEC-11, SEC-12`
- Affects: [SWM-13](#swm-13)
- Recommendation: A wake is a request in the runner's inbox; the runner checks it against the card's wake policy and budget and starts the unit (SWM-13 says so).
- Resolved: swarm phase 4 (2026-10-30): a wake is a request in the runner's inbox, checked against the card's wake policy and budget (SWM-13); confirmed 2026-11-01 by tests/test_swarm_board.py::test_a_request_wakes_an_on_request_agent_within_its_budget

### FND-24

**A second store for the same artifacts** (medium incoherence, fixed)

Proposals, builds, Claude tasks and runs live in missions.sqlite, crew findings in crew JSON files and purchase proposals as files. The design adds a Ledger with its own database and migrates them, but does not say when the old stores and their writers stop. Two stores of the same thing drift apart, as the docs and the code did (FND-05 to FND-09).

- Evidence: `/srv/ai/missions/missions.sqlite tables builds, claude_tasks, proposals, runs`; `design doc, rollout phase 1 ('migrate in as artifacts')`
- Affects: [SWM-03](#swm-03), [SWM-35](#swm-35)
- Recommendation: Migrate one kind at a time in one step and remove its old writer in the same phase (SWM-35), after a shadow week (SWM-87).
- Resolved: feat/swarm-switch, 2026-10-29 (ideas live only in the Ledger; the proposals table is read-only history; builds and purchases keep their approval stores and are linked by ref)

### FND-25

**The Claude Code agents are invisible to the gateway's records** (medium incoherence, fixed)

The design takes cost from LiteLLM spend per key and replay sets from Phoenix traces, and MIS-16 takes cost per run from the gateway's records. The builder, the health doctor, the maintainability review and Claude tasks run Claude Code on the subscription outside the gateway, so neither LiteLLM nor Phoenix has their calls, and their scorecards, replay sets and cost would be empty.

- Evidence: `requirement CST-02 (Claude Code runs outside the gateway)`; `requirement MIS-16 (partial)`; `design doc, 'Scorecards' (cost from LiteLLM spend per key)`
- Affects: [SWM-17](#swm-17), [SWM-79](#swm-79), [SWM-81](#swm-81), [SWM-82](#swm-82), [MIS-16](#mis-16)
- Recommendation: The run contract records each run's inputs and usage itself (Claude Code's JSON result carries usage), and Claude Code's OpenTelemetry export is pointed at Phoenix.
- Resolved: feat/swarm-switch, 2026-10-29 (Claude Code runs go to Phoenix as traces from the runtime record, which already holds their tokens per model call)

### FND-29

**The price watch repeats a reached target as news** (medium defect, fixed)

On 2026-10-29 the speaker's price stayed at 68.61 EUR, under the 69 EUR target. Every price reading proposed again, each run said 'wait, sold out', and Telegram got ten messages (10:18 to 19:32). The 6-hour timer lived only in the runner's memory, and every deploy restarted the runner, so a reading came right after each restart.

- Evidence: `missions.sqlite runs 30-39 of home-assistant-voice-speaker-one-to-try, all trigger 'price target reached', summary 68.61 EUR`; `ops/shopping.py tick(): _ticks in memory only; no memory of the price already alerted`
- Affects: [SHP-04](#shp-04), [MIS-04](#mis-04)
- Recommendation: Remember the check time and the alerted price on the proposal; a mission does not run again on an identical event within 6 hours.
- Resolved: feat/swarm-phase3, 2026-10-29 (watched_at and alerted_total on the proposal; the runner's loop guard on identical events)

### FND-31

**Every swarm sync failed at the wake step after the phase 4 deploy** (medium defect, fixed)

The usage-limit check read state.json's builds as a dict ({builds: [...]}); the runner writes them as a list, so wake() raised on every minute's swarm sync from the deploy until the fix. Publishing and the activity feed ran before it and kept working; wakes and the owner's Board notifications did not. The unit tests passed a fake limit function, so the real reader was never run.

- Evidence: `journal swarm-sync.service 2026-10-30 01:00-01:03: AttributeError: 'list' object has no attribute 'get' (swarm_client.py limited)`
- Affects: [SWM-53](#swm-53)
- Recommendation: Read the list (and tolerate the old shape), and test limited() on the runner's real state.json shape.
- Resolved: swarm/phase4-gaps, 2026-10-30 (tests/test_swarm_phase4.py::test_the_usage_limit_is_read_from_the_runners_real_state)

### FND-39

**A long run input became invalid JSON in the Ledger** (medium defect, fixed)

ledger.py stored run inputs and outputs as json.dumps(...)[:40000] (and experiment details at 60,000): a longer value became a cut JSON string, and the next replays() or experiment read would fail to parse it.

- Evidence: `images/swarm/ledger.py run_start, finish, context (inputs), experiment (detail) before fix/no-truncation`
- Affects: [SWM-82](#swm-82)
- Recommendation: Bound JSON so it still parses (textcap.bounded_json, marked as cut).

### FND-41

**The chat's New chat button did nothing on a new chat** (medium defect, fixed)

The chat header's + linked to #/chat. On a new chat the address is already #/chat, so no hashchange fired and nothing happened; the attach button next to the message box was a second + with another job.

### FND-42

**A mission run reads and writes five overlapping memories** (medium incoherence, fixed)

A mission run's opening holds its working notes (NOTES.md), its journal (standing notes and the last entries), the top wiki matches of a search over every collection, a Hindsight recall and, through its tools, the swarm's pack, with no order of authority; it then writes its notes, a report, a journal entry, its wiki and (through the journal) Hindsight facts. The notes and the journal's standing notes say much the same; Hindsight copies every journal entry.

- Evidence: `ops/missions.py TASK_PROMPT and file_task_result`; `ops/journal.py brief() and the Hindsight retain`; `the owner's review, chat 2026-10-30`
- Affects: [MEM-05](#mem-05), [MEM-06](#mem-06)
- Recommendation: One opening per run in a fixed order and budget; mission notes as journal standing notes; personal facts only in Hindsight.
- Resolved: memory/coherent, 2026-10-30: one opening per run (ops/journal.py brief), the working state in the journal, no journal copies in Hindsight (tests/test_memory_coherent.py)

### FND-47

### FND-62

**The health board's notes were mostly false alarms** (medium defect, fixed)

28 of the board's 30 issues were notes, nearly all from two checks that misjudged valid calls. swarm_context takes only optional arguments, so {} is a correct call, yet obs_core.problems() reported it as called without arguments, and telemetry.py counted every {} call as arrived without arguments (gateway problem; run make probe-tools). The partial-read note fired on every run that fetched a long page, even when the agent read on.

- Evidence: `/api/state health on 2026-10-30 (30 items with issues)`; `images/mission-control/obs_core.py:306`; `ops/telemetry.py:182`
- Affects: [OBS-07](#obs-07), [OBS-10](#obs-10)
- Recommendation: Judge an empty call against the tool's own arguments (one list, checked against every tool), and count a cut-off only when nothing read on.
- Resolved: fix/health-signals, 2026-10-30: obs_core.NO_ARGS and empty_call(), unfollowed_cuts(); telemetry uses empty_call

### FND-87

**The Alexa sign-in in Home Assistant spun forever** (medium defect, fixed)

Adding Alexa Devices in Home Assistant 2026.10.4 never finished: its library, aioamazondevices 16.3.2, expects Amazon's old sign-in form with e-mail and password together, while Amazon now asks for the e-mail first on a form that posts to a relative /ax/claim address; the request failed inside the library and the setup showed no error. Upstream fixed it (aioamazondevices #1194, 16.9.1 and later); Home Assistant ships it with 2026.11.

- Evidence: `homeassistant container on the NAS, 1 Nov 2026: 2026.10.4 with aioamazondevices 16.3.2`; `aioamazondevices issues #1188 and #1236 (the same version pair, closed: the fix ships in 2026.11)`
- Affects: [HSE-05](#hse-05)
- Recommendation: Run Home Assistant 2026.11.0b2 (aioamazondevices 17.2.3) until 2026.11.0 is out, then pin the release; the config folder was backed up on the NAS before the switch.
- Resolved: fix/ha-alexa-login, 2026-11-01: nas/homeassistant/compose.yaml pins 2026.11.0b2; deployed with make ha-deploy; the sign-in form opens on it. The owner's own sign-in with Amazon is the last proof.

A row on the Agents map opened the Chatroom on a conversation id (calls:mission+scout) that none of the room's lines carry, so it showed nothing; the filter was kept in the page's memory, so the Chatroom tab stayed empty on later visits until a reload. The map itself was a list of rows, the team's cards opened nothing and team agents had no page.

- Evidence: `the owner's screenshots, 1 Nov 2026 09:14-09:18`; `/api/swarm/room?hours=168: 699 of 705 lines without ref.context`
- Affects: [SWM-104](#swm-104), [SWM-107](#swm-107)
- Recommendation: A pair is two picked agents; Activity's filters come from the address on every visit; the map is agents as dots and lines that open Activity; every team agent has one page.
- Resolved: fix/agents-one-page, 2026-11-01: swarm.js swActivityFromUrl(), swMapCard(), swAgentPanel(); app.js AGENT_VIEWS; tests/test_swarm_views.py

### FND-92

**The model judge said "not met" on code that passed every case** (medium defect, fixed)

Trial semver, second run (task #91, 1 Nov 2026): the delivered library passed 351 of 351 answer-key cases, yet the model judge rated the run not met: it read only the builder's summary, which named 114 passing tests but did not list a test per rule, and judged the summary, not the code. A judge that reads text cannot tell working code from a modest report.

- Evidence: `/srv/vault/trials/semver/semver-20261101-1144.md`; `task_occ of task 91: verdict not met`
- Affects: [TRL-03](#trl-03), [TSK-17](#tsk-17)
- Recommendation: For coding work the judge runs the checks (the build's tests, a trial's scorer) and reads their results; a model judges only what no program can (phase 10b). The trial report flags every disagreement meanwhile.
- Resolved: night/w1, 2026-11-01: ops/staffing.py evidence_of() puts each build's checks, the check steps and the opened pages after the answer; prompts/shared.md TASK_JUDGE trusts them; tests/test_staffing.py

### FND-93

**The card guard took a gate result's file name for a card number** (medium defect, fixed)

From 31 Oct 2026 14:13 every requirements sync to the swarm was refused (1,117 times in a day) as carrying a payment card: TSK-03 and TSK-06 name gate results like eval/results/20261031-140852-tasks-gate.json, and the 14 digits of that stamp pass the Luhn check. The swarm's copy of the requirements stopped updating, and the Checker failed every hour on the refusal count.

- Evidence: `swarm.db refusals: actor system, op requirements_sync, 1,117 rows from 2026-10-31 14:13`; `journalctl crew-checker.service, 1 Nov 2026`
- Affects: [SWM-68](#swm-68)
- Recommendation: A card is one run of 13 to 19 digits or groups of 3 to 6 digits; a date-time stamp is neither. The Checker's alert names the operation and reason, and goes quiet once no refusal came for 2 hours (it otherwise kept failing for a day after the fix).
- Resolved: fix/timeline-card-guard and fix/checker-refusals, 2026-11-01: images/swarm/ledger.py CARD_RE and refusal_causes; ops/checker.py signals; tests/test_swarm_ledger.py, tests/test_checker.py

### FND-112

**The checker reports a fault it found as its own failure** (low incoherence, fixed)

The crew's checker ends its run as failed whenever the swarm shows a fault (ops/checker.py run raises), so the health board's alert reaches the owner. From 31 Oct 15:24 to 1 Nov 14:36 it failed 25 of its runs because the Ledger refused the requirements sync 1,128 times (the card-number guard's false positive on a file name's stamp, FND-93, fixed 1 Nov). The alarm was right, but the job's failure count mixes a broken job with a faithful report: its page showed one run in four failed, and failure rates are what the planned licences (AUT-02) and the self-pause after three failed runs read.

- Evidence: `runtime.db crew:checker: 95 runs, 25 failed, each ending 'system had N refused swarm writes in 24 h'`; `swarm.db refusals: system requirements_sync 1,128, 31 Oct 13:13 to 1 Nov 12:22`; `docs/PROBES.md P1`
- Affects: [AUT-02](#aut-02)
- Recommendation: End the run as done with the signals as its result and raise them through the health board's alert (crew.ALERTS), so a failed run means the job itself broke.
- Resolved: fix/p6, 2026-11-02: the checker's run ends done with its faults kept; crew.checker_faults raises each as a swarm-fault alert on its card, cleared by its next clean run

### FND-113

**Every nightly journey check left a swarm run "never finished"** (low defect, fixed)

The crew wrapper opens a swarm run for every crew job (as system) and finishes it as the job. The journeys have no swarm card, so the Ledger refused each finish ("no card for 'journeys'") without a trace in its refusals, and every night's run stayed open: the health board said a run of the journey checks never finished and the Ledger labelled it failed (FC1), though the runs passed (186 checks on 2 Nov).

- Evidence: `swarm.db runs: crew:journeys:20261101-084733 and crew:journeys:20261102-041227 unfinished, labelled failed`; `swarm_client finish as journeys: {'ok': False, 'refused': "no card for 'journeys'"}`
- Affects: [SWM-64](#swm-64)
- Recommendation: Open a swarm run only for a crew job with a swarm card; close the two runs on record.
- Resolved: ops/battle, 2026-11-02: crew.run opens and finishes a swarm run only when the job has a card; the two runs closed with their results

### FND-114

**Two Conductor scout steps that start together can be filed under each other** (low defect, fixed)

A Conductor step's own scout run cannot be told its step (Hermes runs in one resident process), so the run record files it under the live step of that agent most recently active. Probe P2 ran two tasks' scout steps in parallel and each went under its own task, partly because they started 10 s apart; two that start within the same second can be swapped, moving one task's sources and tokens to the other.

- Evidence: `docs/PROBES.md P2: tasks 116 and 117`; `images/mission-control/runrecord.py _infer_ref`
- Affects: [OBS-18](#obs-18)
- Recommendation: Put the step's run id at the head of its prompt so the run record matches it exactly, through the prompt replay gate.
- Resolved: fix/step-ref, 2026-11-02: a Hermes step's prompt starts with [run: task:N:step:i]; the run record files a run whose title names a known run under it exactly, before any guess by time

On the live scorecards it would have opened an agent change for the maintainability review whose target (valid answers) was already at its best, so its experiment could never pass.

### FND-35

**The requirements sync counted unchanged requirements as created** (low defect, fixed)

The second sync after the phase 5 deploy reported created 346 when one requirement had changed: an unchanged requirement's write is a replay of its first one (same idempotency key), and the replay's stored result says created. The Ledger itself was right (347 artifacts, 347 keys); only the count shown on the Improve tab and in the sync log was wrong.

- Evidence: `swarm-sync log 2026-10-30 06:47:22: requirements created 346, moved 0; Ledger: 347 requirement artifacts, 347 distinct keys`
- Affects: [SWM-20](#swm-20)
- Recommendation: Count created and updated from whether the write is new, not from its stored result.
- Resolved: swarm/phase5-fix2, 2026-10-30 (tests/test_swarm_phase5.py::test_a_requirement_file_change_creates_or_updates_its_artifact_and_a_violated_one_is_open)

- Resolved: fix/ux-review, 2026-10-30: the header lists the failed jobs by name, each a link

### FND-61

**The wiki maps overlap their names and still show archived wikis** (low defect, fixed)

On the Wiki tab's map (How they connect) the names of neighbouring bubbles overlap, and the archived briefing's wiki is still drawn, so Job report appears twice.

- Evidence: `UX review 2026-10-30, #/wiki at 375 px`; `server.py wiki_roots (archived missions included)`
- Affects: [MEM-09](#mem-09)
- Recommendation: Put each name where it covers no other name or bubble, and leave out wikis of archived missions.
- Resolved: fix/empty-and-wikimap and fix/wikimap-room, 2026-10-30: wikiMapLabels picks below, above, right or left, and the map grows until every name has a free place (live, 8 wikis at 375 px: none covered); archived wikis in a fold

### FND-63

**Agents call swarm_context although their opening holds it** (low incoherence, fixed)

Missions call swarm_context several times a run (once with the same arguments three times), although the opening of every run already holds its answer and says not to call it.

- Evidence: `health notes on 2026-10-30 (RAM upgrade, Job report, Home Assistant speaker)`; `ops/journal.py:303`
- Affects: [SWM-16](#swm-16)
- Recommendation: Make a repeated swarm_context in the same run answer from the opening, or drop the tool from runs that get the opening.
- Resolved: night/w1, 2026-11-01: images/swarm/swarm.py answer() gives a repeated pack within 20 min as a short note (the runner's opening always built); MCP instructions say once; tests/test_swarm_service.py

### FND-70

**Gap notes that no longer said what is true** (low drift, fixed)

OBS-16's gap named four Telegram senders that now go through notify.sh and missed three that send their own; CST-09 stayed partial for a German topic that had been archived; FND-28 stayed open although fixed two days earlier.

- Evidence: `grep of Telegram senders (2026-10-30)`; `state.json: beta-materials-aspi-unternehmens-news archived`; `dae7d13`
- Affects: [OBS-16](#obs-16)
- Recommendation: Re-check every partial, violated and open item in each review, as this one did.
- Resolved: fix/req-sync, 2026-10-30: the three corrected; the review's sweep 2 re-checked all 37

### FND-73

**Long pages without a way across, and identifiers where names belong** (low defect, fixed)

At 375 px the Stack page ran 19 screens (17 sections, nothing to jump with) and Home 13 (a feed of 40); the greeting said owner, the start of the e-mail address; Scheduled jobs listed unit names such as crew-checker; a Code link without a repository said No repository called .

- Evidence: `review 2026-10-30, sweep 4 (#/system, #/, #/repo at 375 px)`
- Affects: [APP-26](#app-26)
- Recommendation: Section links on the Stack page, a feed of 12, a display name or none, job names, a redirect to the list.
- Resolved: fix/ux-polish, 2026-10-30: all five

### FND-91

**A plan's build also shows as a task of its own** (low defect, fixed)

The trial's build #11 appeared on the Tasks board as task #89 (source door) beside its plan's task #88: the board's sync registers every build as a door task, also one a plan's build step started.

- Evidence: `tasks 88 (trial:semver, plan) and 89 (door, build 11), 1 Nov 2026`
- Affects: [TRL-05](#trl-05)
- Recommendation: The sync leaves out a build that a task's build step started (task_steps.resume names it).
- Resolved: feat/trials-10b, 2026-11-01: ops/conductor.py step_refs; ops/tasks.py discover() leaves them out; tests/test_conductor.py

## ACR AI change requests

### ACR-01

**Every idea says where it came from** · should · functional · **implemented**

Each idea shall name the run and the agent that first gave evidence for it, how many runs saw it, and the task that run belongs to (an idea written outside a recorded run, its writer's duty); the idea's page shall name that run and task, its card shall name its task, a task's page shall link to the ideas from its runs, and the Network shall mark a task that produced ideas.

- Parents: [SN-18](#sn-18)
- Sources: [DEC-47](#dec-47), `owner, 8 Nov 2026: in the idea still be able to reconstruct how they got generated`
- Verified by: test: `tests/test_swarm_ledger.py::test_an_idea_names_the_run_and_the_agent_that_first_saw_it`, `tests/test_tasks_tab.py::test_each_idea_is_tied_to_the_task_whose_run_first_saw_it`, `tests/test_tasks_tab.py::test_an_idea_shows_where_it_came_from_and_a_task_its_ideas`
- Trace: `images/swarm/ledger.py`, `images/swarm/swarm.py`, `images/mission-control/runrecord.py`, `images/mission-control/server.py`, `images/mission-control/web/static/app-base.js`, `images/mission-control/web/static/swarm.js`, `images/mission-control/web/static/task.js`, `images/mission-control/web/static/app-tasks.js`, `images/mission-control/web/static/app-wiki-code.js`
- Refined by: [ACR-02](#acr-02)

### ACR-02

**The Editor sorts every open idea, and changes nothing yet** · should · functional · **implemented**

A crew duty, the Editor, shall once a day give every open idea one verdict with its reason (into a named change request, held, covered by named planned work, or dropped), checked by a program (an open idea, one of the four verdicts, a reason, a request's title for "into"); the ideas it could not sort shall be listed; its sorting shall show on each idea and on the Ideas view, and no idea shall change state because of it.

- Parents: [ACR-01](#acr-01)
- Sources: [DEC-47](#dec-47), `owner, 8 Nov 2026: We need an agent that will filter out the good ideas. At the moment it is too spammy.`
- Verified by: test: `tests/test_editor.py::test_the_editor_keeps_only_verdicts_it_can_check_and_groups_requests`, `tests/test_editor.py::test_the_editor_s_context_holds_the_ideas_the_owner_s_reasons_and_planned_work`, `tests/test_editor.py::test_a_pass_saves_its_sorting_and_changes_nothing`, `tests/test_editor.py::test_the_editor_is_a_crew_duty_with_its_card_units_and_prompt`, `tests/test_editor.py::test_each_idea_and_the_ideas_view_show_the_editor_s_sorting`
- Trace: `ops/editor.py`, `prompts/editor.md`, `ops/systemd/crew-editor.service`, `n8n/workflows/crew-editor.json`, `config/agents.yaml`, `ops/crew.py`, `images/mission-control/server.py`, `images/mission-control/server.a2a.py`

## APP App

#### APP-01

**Phone first** · must · quality · **implemented**

Every screen shall work at 375 px width without horizontal scrolling and on a desktop with the side navigation from 960 px, and the tab bar shall spread over however many tabs there are; no screen has an app top bar, each starts with its own title (the chat with its own bar), every section including Stack is in the tab bar, the live state is a dot on Stack, a bar shows at the top only while the app is offline or an update waits, and a page's main action fits on its title row; a card that stands for a page opens it on a tap, and an agent's text in a card is its formatted markdown.

- Parents: [SN-06](#sn-06)
- Sources: `images/mission-control/web/AGENTS.md`
- Verified by: test: `tests/test_now_card.py::test_the_running_card_opens_its_mission_and_folds_each_finding_as_markdown`, `tests/test_now_card.py::test_a_tap_on_a_card_opens_its_page_but_not_on_its_own_links_or_folds`, `tests/test_app_layout.py::test_an_icon_in_text_is_one_line_high_and_no_page_has_an_app_top_bar`, `tests/test_app_layout.py::test_the_chat_has_one_top_bar_on_a_phone_with_the_live_state_in_it`, `tests/test_app_layout.py::test_the_wiki_s_new_button_is_one_line_on_the_title_row`, `tests/test_app_routes.py::test_the_tab_bar_spreads_however_many_tabs_there_are`, `tests/test_app_layout.py::test_the_section_headings_keep_their_gap_and_stay_on_one_line`, `tests/test_app_layout.py::test_the_swarm_s_views_are_under_agents_on_every_screen`, `tests/test_app_layout.py::test_new_chat_starts_afresh_and_attaching_is_a_clip`
- Trace: `images/mission-control/web/static/app.css`
- Findings: [FND-40](#fnd-40), [FND-41](#fnd-41), [FND-82](#fnd-82)

#### APP-02

**Every screen opens cold** · must · quality · **implemented**

Every app route shall render when it is opened directly (a link, a notification, a reload), without first visiting another screen.

**No call of the app waits forever** · must · functional · **implemented**

Every call the app makes shall end within its time limit (45 s, 30 s for a request, 15 s for each check of a slow request's answer); a request that gets no answer shall say so and, for a new task, tell the owner to look under Tasks before trying again; a check of a slow request that stalls shall be retried, and a slow request still running after 5 minutes shall say that it goes on. #### APP-35

**Every task has its own page** · must · functional · **implemented**

Each task shall have its own page (#/task/<id>), opened from every notification, list and link that names the task, showing where it stands and why, its answer, its definition of done with the verdict and the judge's reasons, its steps with their results, the agents that worked on it with their part (planned it, a step, called in by another agent) and what each did (runs, tool and model calls, tokens, time), a timeline of when each agent worked and of every action, what its agents read from and kept in memory, and every run of a recurring task; an agent's page shall list the tasks it worked on and its part in each.

#### APP-36

**A task's page tells its whole story, the result first** · must · functional · **implemented**

A task's page shall open on its result and then tell how it was done, most important first, each part opening a tab with the whole of it: who worked on it as a map of the agents (each agent a bubble coloured by its part and sized by its own tokens, joined by its hand-offs, calls and starts, each tappable for its part, runs, tokens, cost, models, tools and memory use, and the same in words); the definition of done with the verdict; what went wrong (failed steps, retries, failed or silent runs, errors, points not met); its sources (read, cited, by site, by step and agent); its memory use by store (wikis, notes, journals, long-term memory, the swarm's board, lessons) with what was read and kept, including what the stack read or kept around the agents (a refinement's wiki, a briefing's topic wiki pages, the answer); what it made (the answer, reports, wiki pages, files, findings, deliveries); its cost and time by agent and model; and the plan. A recurring task shall show one run at a time (its newest, or the one picked), and a task handed to Claude Code, a build, a chat or a mission shall show its agents too. The map's colours shall pass the colour-blind check on both themes.

#### APP-38

**Home is your desk: one result per outcome, and it never moves under your finger** · must · functional · **implemented**

Home shall hold the box to give a task or ask, then, in its tabs, what needs the owner, what is at work now (tasks among it, with their steps and agents), and Results: one card per outcome the owner asked for or the owner's rules started (a briefing, a task's answer, an alert's answer, research, a shopping step, a failure), each with its kind, its headline, who made it, its first lines and its page; a rule that fires and the task it starts are one Alert card. The stack's own work (health, deploys, the Ledger, crew jobs, trials, probes) shall not be among the results. While the owner is scrolled down or touching the screen, Home's updates shall wait behind a button.

#### APP-39

**What you make in a chat, you reach from the chat, and it leads back** · must · functional · **implemented**

Every chat answer in which Hermes made something (a mission, a build, a Claude task, a repository, a note in the vault, an e-mail) shall show it under the answer as a card with its kind, its name, its state now (being created, queued, running, done, waits for you, failed) and its result's headline, opening its page in one tap; a change to an item (paused, triggers set, sent back) shall be one line. The chat's header shall count what it made (Made here · N) and list it, the chat list shall show it as icons, and the item's page shall say From a chat with a link back to the answer that made it. A one-off item shall say in its chat when it ends; a recurring one shall not. What the chat made shall be read from the run record's tool results and their ids, never guessed by time.

The app's name shall come from the deployment (APP_NAME, "AI Stack" when unset); its page, its installed name, its guide and the stack's messages shall say it, and nothing the owner sees shall say another.
